# LearnBuddy — Repository-wide Audit

Audit of branch `claude/exciting-galileo-nl25uq` at commit `62a6028` (2026-09-27). Scope: the whole
monorepo (`apps/api`, `apps/mobile`, `packages/*`, `infra/supabase`, docs, tests, deploy config).

## How this audit was done

1. **Reconstruct.** The product model, 54 features, 19 user journeys and declared non-goals were
   rebuilt from code and docs, independent of what the docs claim.
2. **Find.** Subsystem reviewers and cross-cutting lenses (journeys, state and concurrency,
   lifecycle, privacy, security, agent and tooling, UX, contradictions, completeness) produced
   668 raw findings (491 after merging duplicates; 10 refuted).
3. **Verify.** Each finding was checked by independent verifiers across layers (contract, DB,
   API, app, docs). Where possible they ran code: `parseNumericInput`/`ruleCheck` calls and
   temporary integration tests on the real Postgres harness, deleted afterwards. 28 hypotheses
   were reproduced at runtime (`repro-*`). Every critical claim got a second, independent opinion.
4. **Merge.** Duplicates were merged into canonical findings; reproductions are cited as runtime
   proof next to the descriptive finding. Refuted claims are not reported.

Finding ids in backticks (e.g. `p2-NM-01`) are stable references across all sections.
Confidence is one of: _Confirmed (runtime-reproduced)_, _Confirmed (code-traced)_,
_Partly confirmed_, _Needs runtime verification_. Nothing here was checked on a physical device,
on the hosted Supabase project or on a Vercel deployment; those claims are collected in §17.

## Executive summary

| Severity | Count                        |
| -------- | ---------------------------- |
| Critical | 7 (C-1 … C-7)                |
| High     | 37 (H-1 … H-37)              |
| Medium   | 85 (M-1 … M-85)              |
| Low      | 263 (tabled by theme in §6b) |

- **The answer check is the critical defect.** All 7 criticals sit in the deterministic rule check
  (`apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts` + `packages/shared-math`). Its "incorrect" verdict
  cannot be overruled by the model, so correct answers are ruled wrong (`0,125` for key `0.125`
  in de/fr/es/it) and wrong ones ruled right (`242` for `240`, signs and operators ignored, German
  capitalisation and ß ignored). This feeds hints, worked solutions, FSRS scheduling and test results.
- **Core journeys dead-end.** Homework help becomes unreachable after 12 h and "Beenden" is final;
  practice sessions with every question answered are invisible; onboarding can trap a parent at
  the PIN or consent step; a child profile aged 16/17 returns a 500.
- **Privacy promises outrun the code.** Account erasure parks silently after 3 failures and never
  completes above 1000 photos; "deleting" a sheet only archives it; the Supabase link points to
  London, not the EU region the privacy doc promises; iOS speech leaves the device for a second locale.
- **Security.** Changing the parent PIN has no lockout (brute-forceable), and the admin-session
  lockout counter loses concurrent updates.
- **Resilience.** An auth refresh 5xx signs the learner out and wipes the outbox and photo drafts;
  a verifier outage is reported as 401.
- **Contact rules.** In-app outreach is not counted toward contact caps; a reminder the child agreed
  to can vanish silently.
- **Deployment.** `vercel.json` is not a valid deploy config for this API.
- **What holds.** No `any`, no stubs, no test fakes in production paths, zod validation on every
  model decision, context fence on the main path, clock handling well tested at unit level.

None of the 19 traced journeys holds end to end: 3 break, 16 hold partly. §16 lists 11 immediate,
12 near-term and 9 structural work packages plus 14 product decisions.

## 1. What This Application Actually Does

This section is rebuilt from the code at HEAD `62a6028` (2026-09-26). It is not taken from the README or the design docs. Where the docs promise something the code does not do, the difference is listed in §1.4 with the finding id that proves it.

### 1.1 Who uses it

| Actor                                                 | What the code lets them do                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Where it is enforced                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Learner, minor (<16)**. Reference persona: Lena, 12 | Chat with Buddy (text, dictation, conversation mode). Photograph worksheets and homework. Practise. Correct what Buddy remembers. Reduce contact. Rename the profile and change its language. Sign out. She **cannot** loosen contact, export, delete, or set or change the PIN. | `apps/api/src/http/context.ts` `actorOf` / `assertAccountHolder`: a minor counts as the account holder only while a valid `x-admin-token` is present. |
| **Learner, adult self-profile (≥16)**                 | Same app. `actorOf` always grants account-holder rights, with no PIN.                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `context.ts` (`isMinor` false → account_holder)                                                                                                       |
| **Account holder / parent**                           | Signs up (Supabase Auth, e-mail + password) and consents to the privacy text. Creates exactly **one** learner profile. Sets a 4-digit PIN. Unlocks gated steps with a 10-minute HMAC admin token. Manages contact settings, export, deletion, login credentials and sign-out.    | `modules/identity/routes.ts`, `identity/model.ts` (scrypt PIN, HMAC token)                                                                            |
| **Buddy (the model)**                                 | Interprets messages and plans. It acts only through registered act tools on aliases (`g1`, `st2`, `m3`, `f1`) and DaySpec/UntilSpec. Code re-validates every action.                                                                                                             | `modules/buddy/registry.ts`, `tools.ts`, `apply.ts`                                                                                                   |
| **Scheduler tick**                                    | Runs extraction, Buddy checks, delivery, receipts, photo purge and account deletion. Triggered every minute by pg_cron → pg_net → `POST /internal/tick`.                                                                                                                         | `modules/scheduler/tick.ts`, `0001_baseline.sql:587-606`, `0002_scheduler.sql:16`                                                                     |
| **Operator**                                          | Sets `TICK_SECRET`, the Vault secrets `lb_api_url` / `lb_tick_secret`, `PUSH_BACKEND` and the Vertex project. Reads `/health`.                                                                                                                                                   | `apps/api/src/config.ts`, ADR 0004 §Transition                                                                                                        |

Only one learner per account is a deliberate constraint (unique `account_id`). Multi-child households are not supported.

### 1.2 The core loop, as implemented

1. **Onboarding.** `welcome.tsx` (Supabase sign-up/sign-in) → `consent.tsx` (`POST /account`, consent version must equal `CONSENT_VERSION`) → `profile.tsx` (`POST /learner`, then `PUT /account/pin` for a child) → `buddy.tsx`. The gate in `app/index.tsx` decides the route from `GET /me`.
2. **Talk to Buddy.** The home is one screen: orb, starter ring, composer with mic and camera, one "now" card, an optional decision card, and the last six thread messages with action cards and "Rückgängig". Unsent photos and unread pages are now told as chat bubbles (commit `62a6028`). A message goes to `POST /buddy/messages`. The server stores it idempotently and renders the learner's situation as a STATE block with aliases. It asks Gemini (Vertex, EU) for a zod-validated `TurnDecision`, with up to two read-only lookup rounds. It applies the decision all-or-nothing behind `context_version` and streams the reply over SSE. A reply is streamed and spoken while it is written only when the decision changes nothing.
3. **Photograph material.** `capture.tsx` downscales and runs a quality check on the device. The app then reserves the material idempotently, PUTs the photos to Supabase Storage via signed URLs, and submits. The server queues an `extract_material` job and runs it at once through `deps.background`. One Gemini call turns the photos into items, a subject, page reports and a `material_ready` event, which wakes a Buddy check that prepares practice. Photos are purged 7 days after reading.
4. **Practise.** `practice/[id].tsx` shows one question at a time. The server grades authoritatively: rule checks first (`evaluate.ts`), then the tutor model (`tutor.ts`) under enforced invariants. There is a hint ladder, a worked solution after three misses, and one FSRS review per closed question. Modes: practice, explain, test, vocab, speak (pronunciation), help (homework). Typed answers go through an on-device outbox, so each one is recorded once even offline.
5. **Homework help.** A photo taken with `purpose=homework` becomes a _help_ session. It never reveals the solution, and the tutor's replies go through a leak guard. It is excluded from spaced repetition. In the app it is reachable only through the 12-hour resume card (`home.ts:137-141`).
6. **Goals and steps.** "Mathearbeit am Freitag" becomes `plan_exam`: a goal plus countdown jobs at d-5, d-3 and d-1 and a follow-up. `plan_step` / `prepare_practice` create steps (planned → prepared → in_progress → done/skipped/cancelled). An "agreed" step with a time gets a deterministic `step_due` reminder that makes no model call.
7. **Proactive outreach.** Checks (countdown, follow-up, material_ready, session_finished, checkin_requested, routine) ask the model for a `CheckDecision`, or fall back to fixed templates. A proposed message goes through a pure contact policy (`policy.ts`: opt-in, pause, quiet hours, window, 1/day and 4/week caps, 72 h topic dedupe, 48 h unanswered gate). It is then delivered by Expo push, or `in_app` as a thread post. **`PUSH_BACKEND` defaults to `disabled` (`config.ts:84`)**, so in the shipped configuration every outreach is an in-app thread message.
8. **Parents' area.** Settings → "Für Eltern": PIN, e-mail/password change, JSON export, deletion with a 7-day hold, sign-out. Contact settings allow anyone to tighten. Loosening needs the PIN for a minor (`routes.ts` `loosens()`).
9. **Export and deletion.** `GET /account/export` assembles the learner tables as JSON, delivered through the OS share sheet. `POST /account/deletion` sets `deletion_due_at = now + 7 d`. The scheduler's `delete_account` job removes the storage objects and the auth user, and the rows cascade.

### 1.3 What runs where by default

- `LLM_BACKEND=vertex`; with no model configured, `DisabledGateway` (`production.ts:19`). Turns then fail with `model_unavailable`, checks use templates, and practice records "kann ich gerade nicht prüfen".
- `PUSH_BACKEND=disabled` (`config.ts:84`). This is a declared non-goal until legal review, so it is not a defect in itself. It does change how the contact caps behave (see `p2-in-app-contact-not-counted`).
- The web build is labelled "development and browser tests only" (`lib/auth/session.ts:2`), yet `app.json` ships web configuration.

What the docs describe and the code does honour: aliases and DaySpec (the model never writes ids or dates), quote provenance, the context-fence compare-and-set in `apply.ts:82`, the `buddy_decisions` audit, job leases with CAS finish, the photo upload via signed URLs, and per-learner daily model budgets.

## 2. Architecture and Runtime Model

### 2.1 Components

```text
┌──────────────────────── apps/mobile (Expo Router; iOS / Android / web) ───────────────────────┐
│ app/: index(gate) welcome consent profile buddy(home) capture practice/[id] talk library      │
│       material/[id] memory history settings pin reset-password                                │
│ lib/api: client.ts (Bearer, refresh single-flight, x-timezone, x-admin-token)                  │
│          endpoints.ts (zod-validated) · queries.ts (TanStack; home poll 60 s / 3 s)            │
│          outbox*.ts (typed answers, ≤50, 7 d) · sse.ts / streamingFetch (turn stream)          │
│ lib/capture (draft 7 d, sent-photo list 24 h) · lib/speech (on-device STT / TTS)              │
│ lib/auth (Supabase Auth, SecureStore/localStorage) · lib/admin*.ts (admin token, memory only) │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │ HTTPS /v1/*  (JSON ≤64 KB; 2 MB     │ Supabase Auth (passwords, refresh)
                │ audio; SSE for turns)               │ Supabase Storage (signed PUT)
┌───────────────▼──────────── apps/api (Hono; Vercel function api/[[...slug]].ts) ──────────────┐
│ http/context.ts  requireUser → requireAccount → requireLearner (upserts timezone) · actorOf   │
│ modules/identity   /me /account /learner /account/pin /admin-session /export /deletion       │
│ modules/buddy      turn · context · lookups · registry · apply · tools · check · policy ·     │
│                    plan(bumpContext) · delivery · events · home · stream                      │
│ modules/materials  create/submit/extract/retry/pages-ok/archive/purge                         │
│ modules/practice   selection · generate · evaluate(rules) · tutor · fsrs · hints · speak      │
│ modules/voice      /voice/transcribe                                                          │
│ modules/scheduler  jobs (lease + CAS) · tick (recover→extract→checks→deliver→receipts→maint.) │
│ llm/ call.ts (budget reserve + llm_calls) → vertex.ts | DisabledGateway                       │
│ push/transport.ts (Expo | Disabled) · storage/gateway.ts · auth/verifier.ts · deps.now()     │
└──────┬──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────┬────────────────────────┬────────────┘
       │ pg (direct, tx, locks)   │ Vertex AI (Gemini,    │ Expo push              │ Supabase
┌──────▼───────────────────┐      │ EU 'eu' endpoint)     │ (off by default)       │ Auth/Storage
│ Postgres (Supabase)      │      ▼                       ▼                        ▼
│ RLS on, no policies      │
│ pg_cron ─1/min─▶ lb_invoke_tick() ─pg_net─▶ POST /internal/tick (x-tick-secret, Vault secrets)
└──────────────────────────┘
```

### 2.2 Modules and their contracts

- **Monorepo.** `apps/api` (Hono on Node), `apps/mobile` (Expo Router), `packages/shared-types` (zod contracts, shipped as raw `.ts`), `packages/shared-math` (numeric parsing), `infra/supabase/migrations` (`0001_baseline.sql` + `0002`–`0011`), `tests/web` (Playwright), `apps/api/evals` (live model).
- **identity.** Account, consent, learner (version CAS), PIN (scrypt), admin token (HMAC, 10 min), export assembly (`privacy.ts`), and the `delete_account` job.
- **buddy.** The conversation core and all proactivity: turns, checks, act tools, contact policy, outreach delivery, events, and the derived home view (`home.ts`).
- **materials.** Photo reservation, submit and extraction. Filing into subjects, merging parts into a root sheet, page reports, and purge scheduling.
- **practice.** Selection (due → new → rest; homework and speak excluded), model-generated sets, rule grading, tutor, FSRS, hints, pronunciation.
- **voice.** Server-side transcription for devices that cannot recognise speech on-device. The recording is never stored.
- **scheduler.** A durable `jobs` table (unique `(kind, dedupe_key)`, `for update skip locked` at `jobs.ts:93`, lease token, max 3 attempts, then parked `failed`) and `runTick` with a 45 s soft budget (`tick.ts:34-37`).

### 2.3 The model seam

`llm/call.ts` is the only entry point. It reserves against `usage_daily` per learner, purpose and day (turn 80, check 8, tutor 300, extraction 12), calls the `LlmGateway`, records tokens, cost and outcome in `llm_calls` (never text), and releases the reservation on `unavailable` / `rate_limited`. `llm/vertex.ts` calls Gemini through the EU multi-region `eu` endpoint and refuses non-EU locations at startup. Output is requested with a zod-derived JSON schema and parsed with zod again. Every model purpose (turn, check, tutor, extraction, topic generation, hints, transcription, pronunciation) has its own prompt, schema, timeout and budget. The model never writes ids or dates. Aliases and DaySpec/UntilSpec are resolved by `context.ts` / `lib/time.ts` in the learner's zone, and DST ambiguity is rejected.

### 2.4 The context fence

`buddy_settings.context_version` is the fence. `buildContext` records the version the model sees. `applyDecision` locks `buddy_settings FOR UPDATE`, compares the version (`apply.ts:82`) and throws `StaleDecision` on mismatch. It then runs every act handler in one transaction, writes `buddy_decisions` and `buddy_actions`, and bumps the version (`apply.ts:215`). Mutation sites outside a decision call `bumpContext` (`plan.ts:14`). Gaps, each proven: the `x-timezone` upsert on every request does not bump (`timezone-header-no-context-bump`, runtime `repro-21`). Per-answer practice progress does not bump either (`answers-do-not-bump-context`). The explicit taps lock rows in the opposite order to apply (`apply-vs-tap-lock-order-deadlock`, runtime `repro-01`; also `p2-J-memory-F4`).

### 2.5 One clock

`deps.now()` (`deps.ts:15-16`) is the only clock in application code. The test harness substitutes a single test clock. The rule-7 letter is not fully kept: `buddy_goals`, `buddy_steps`, `subjects`, `material_photos`, `items` and `practice_turns` still default `created_at` to SQL `now()`, with no behavioural effect found (`goals-steps-created-at-sql-default`). The dev stack runs on the wall clock, so no browser test exercises time-driven screens (`dev-stack-wall-clock-hides-time-driven-ui`).

### 2.6 Request lifecycle (learner message)

1. The app (`Composer` → `sendMessageStreamed`) POSTs `/v1/buddy/messages` with `client_message_id`, `Accept: text/event-stream`, a Bearer token and `x-timezone`.
2. `requireUser` verifies the token with Supabase `auth.getUser` (`auth/verifier.ts:56-57`). `requireLearner` resolves the learner and upserts timezone and `last_seen_at`.
3. `receiveLearnerMessage` inserts the message as `processing` with a `claim_token`. A duplicate replays (done), returns 202 (processing) or re-claims a failed or stalled message. The insert bumps context.
4. `processTurn` makes up to 4 attempts. Each attempt loads the bounded learner state, builds STATE plus dialogue, runs `withLookups` (≤2 rounds × 3 lookups) and `callModel`. Partial reply text streams as SSE `reply` events only if the decision is speakable.
5. The decision goes through `TurnDecision.safeParse`, then `applyDecision` in one transaction (fence, act tools, audit, reply message, bump). A stale fence rebuilds and retries. A rejected action gets one repair round.
6. SSE `done` carries the new home. The app calls `setHome` and the poll continues (60 s idle, 3 s while working).

### 2.7 Job lifecycle (background)

1. pg_cron calls `lb_invoke_tick()` every minute. It reads the Vault secrets and `net.http_post`s `/internal/tick` with `x-tick-secret`. In dev, `server.ts:32` / dev-stack runs a `setInterval` instead.
2. `runTick` runs heartbeat → recover (expired leases, stalled turns → `buddy_turn` jobs, orphaned materials → failed, stale uploads abandoned) → `extract_material` jobs → per-learner `runLearnerJobs` under a 150 s learner lease (`check.ts:44`). Inside that step, `step_due` is deterministic, other triggers go to `CheckDecision` with 3 attempts, a 10-minute retry on provider errors, then fallbacks. Then `sendDueOutreach` (re-checks the rules, then push or `in_app` plus a thread post) → `checkReceipts` → maintenance (`purge_photos`, `delete_account`).
3. Every job finishes with CAS on its lease token. After 3 attempts it is parked `failed`, with no follow-up.
4. Learner actions also kick work immediately: `deps.background` runs extraction after submit, and `finishSession` runs `runLearnerJobs`. On Vercel this is `waitUntil` (`apps/api/api/[[...slug]].ts:9-11`).

### 2.8 Storage, auth, push

- **Storage.** A private bucket `material-photos`, paths under the account id, signed upsert URLs (`storage/gateway.ts`). The app uploads directly. The API verifies the objects exist on submit. Purge happens through jobs.
- **Auth.** Supabase GoTrue, called only from the app (`lib/auth/supabase.ts`). The API verifies bearer tokens and reads the `amr` timestamp for freshness (first PIN within 300 s of a password sign-in). Tokens live in SecureStore, or localStorage on web. The admin token is held in memory only and cleared when settings blurs.
- **Push.** `push/transport.ts` is either `ExpoPush` or `DisabledPush` (the default). Device tokens are globally unique and reassigned to the latest learner on upsert. The only proof of "opened" is the app's call to `/buddy/outreach/:id/opened` after a notification tap.

### 2.9 Mobile app structure

- **Navigation.** Expo Router stack. `app/_layout.tsx` hydrates the session before mounting, clears outbox, drafts and query cache on sign-out, registers the notification-tap listener, flushes the outbox at start and when the device comes online, and wraps the app in `ErrorBoundary`, `ToastHost` and `OfflineFrame`. `app/index.tsx` is the gate: session → `/me` → consent / profile / buddy.
- **Offline.** On phones NetInfo feeds `onlineManager`; on web the browser's online events do. Queries pause behind a calm banner. Typed answers are persisted (`lb.outbox.v1`) and replayed with the same `client_turn_id`. Hints, reveal, flag, finish and speak are not in the outbox.
- **Drafts.** The capture draft `lb.capture.draft.v1` keeps photo files for 7 days. The sent-photos list is kept 24 h for the pages notice.
- **Design system.** `components/lb/` (Btn, Sheet, Toast, OfflineFrame, BuddyOrb, OrbitMenu, Glow) and theme tokens. i18n covers de/en/fr/es/it × 11 namespaces with a parity test.

### 2.10 Deployment

- **API.** Vercel function `apps/api/api/[[...slug]].ts` (`hono/vercel` + `waitUntil`). `apps/api/vercel.json` sets region `fra1`, runtime `nodejs22.x`, `maxDuration` 300, a rewrite of `/v1/(.*)` → `/api/$1`, and security headers. This configuration has never been deployed successfully: the runtime value is rejected, and the catch-all is routed as a single segment (`vercel-deploy-config-invalid`, runtime-proven).
- **Database, Auth, Storage.** Supabase. The committed link state points at `eu-west-2` London (`supabase-region-london-not-eu`). The `lb_invoke_tick` SECURITY DEFINER function stays executable by the anon role (`p2-sec-anon-rpc-lb-invoke-tick`). DB TLS depends on an undocumented `sslmode` in the connection secret (`database-tls-not-enforced`).
- **Scheduler.** pg_cron + pg_net + Vault. This is an operator step and is not automated (ADR 0004 §Transition).

### 2.11 Testing strategy

- **CI** (`.github/workflows/check.yml`) runs typecheck, lint and `pnpm test` against a Postgres 16 service with `LB_REQUIRE_TEST_DB=1`. This covers 14 integration files in `apps/api/src/__tests__/` on real Postgres. Only the model (scripted), push, auth and storage are faked. Unit tests cover time/DST, policy and i18n parity.
- **Browser walkthroughs** (`tests/web/*.spec.ts` via `scripts/web-walkthrough.sh` against `dev-stack.ts`) check onboarding, core loop, modes, offline and the tour, plus the 390×844 / 360×740 fit check. They run only when invoked manually; CI and the pre-commit hook do not run them (`walkthrough-and-fit-never-gated`).
- **Live evals** (`apps/api/evals/{buddy,tutor,speak,speed,stream,lena,modes,voice}`) run against the real model. Six of eight cannot fail (`evals-not-machine-gradable`).
- **Not covered:** the pg_cron → pg_net → tick path (`tick-trigger-path-untested`), real GoTrue confirmation and recovery (`password-reset-and-email-confirm-untested`), native-only paths such as background upload, SecureStore and on-device STT (`no-native-path-coverage`), and SSE through the deployed host (`streaming-through-vercel-unverified`, `repro-18`).

### 2.12 Where the runtime model is fragile

| Fragile point                     | Why                                                                                                                                                                | Finding ids                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Production entry point            | Neither the deploy config nor the cron trigger chain has ever run. Misconfiguration fails silently: the scheduler state stays "unknown" and the app shows nothing. | `vercel-deploy-config-invalid`, `tick-trigger-path-untested`, `system-status-only-partially-surfaced`, `health-ok-despite-failing-tick`, `p2-sec-anon-rpc-lb-invoke-tick` |
| Auth outage becomes sign-out      | The verifier maps every Supabase error to 401. On a refresh 5xx/429 the app clears the session, which also clears the outbox and photo drafts.                     | `p2-api-verifier-outage-becomes-401`, `refresh-5xx-signs-out-and-wipes` (`repro-26`)                                                                                      |
| Turn failure handling             | Non-model exceptions escape the handler, and messages stay `processing` until the 3-minute recovery. Worst case is 12 billed calls per turn.                       | `non-model-error-leaves-message-processing`, `turn-cost-and-stall-window`, `p2-J-stall-recovery-loop-drains-budget`                                                       |
| Fence completeness and lock order | Timezone and answer progress sit outside the fence. Apply and taps lock in opposite orders.                                                                        | `timezone-header-no-context-bump` (`repro-21`), `answers-do-not-bump-context`, `apply-vs-tap-lock-order-deadlock` (`repro-01`)                                            |
| Leases shorter than work          | The check lease is 150 s against a ≈360 s worst case. Extraction and outreach status writes are not lease-fenced.                                                  | `check-lease-shorter-than-runtime` (`repro-17`), `extraction-status-writes-unfenced`, `p2-delivery-status-writes-unfenced`, `send-uncertain-lease-no-thread`              |
| Parked jobs have no follow-up     | Deletion, purge and countdown jobs that fail 3× are parked silently.                                                                                               | `deletion-job-parks-failed-account-stuck` (`repro-24`), `failed-countdown-never-fires-no-fallback`, `recovery-failed-material-never-purged`                               |
| Default push-off configuration    | In-app outreach is not counted, so the policy caps are inert.                                                                                                      | `p2-in-app-contact-not-counted` (`repro-02`)                                                                                                                              |
| Growth                            | The deletion cascade runs through unindexed FKs. `loadBuddyState` subject counts scan all tenants. Jobs and `llm_calls` are never pruned.                          | `p2-account-deletion-cascade-unindexed-fks`, `p2-state-subjects-counts-scan-all-tenants`, `jobs-and-llm-calls-never-pruned-payload-scans`                                 |

---

## 3. Feature & Implementation Completeness

**Sources.** The 54 features come from the `model.md` feature inventory. Statuses come from `final-findings.json` (481 verified findings) and `final-journeys.json`. Of the 19 traced journeys, 3 break: J7 homework help, J8 practice session, and J19 model unavailable or budget exhausted. The other 16 hold only partly.

**Status legend:**

- **complete**: works as documented; only low findings.
- **partly**: the main path works; branches, failure paths or promises do not.
- **broken**: the main path gives wrong results, or is unreachable, for a common case or the default configuration.
- **missing**: not built or not reachable at all.

**Finding ids.** A `repro-NN` in parentheses is the runtime reproduction of the same defect; it is not a separate finding. Declared non-goals (push off, no realtime audio, no live camera guidance) are not counted against a feature.

**Totals:**

| Status   | Features |
| -------- | -------- |
| complete | 2        |
| partly   | 45       |
| broken   | 7        |
| missing  | 0        |

No feature is entirely missing. Several have an entry point missing (voice mode in the chat, homework help after 12 h), and those are counted under partly or broken.

**Reading the table.**

- The machinery is mostly sound: fence, idempotency, audit, leases and tenant scoping.
- The **broken** entries fall into three groups:
  - Rule-based grading (39, 41) and homework help (43). These are the journeys that break in J7 and J8.
  - The parents' PIN (3, 4, 5).
  - Contact caps in the default push-off deployment (23).
- Most **partly** entries fail in the surroundings of a working happy path: expiry windows, parked jobs, silent drops and client-only gates. Two assumptions are never exercised by any test: the production trigger and deploy chain (20), and real-device paths (28, 44).

## 4. Critical Findings

All seven critical findings sit in one place: the deterministic answer check `ruleCheck` in `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts` and the helpers it calls in `packages/shared-math` (`parseNumericInput`, `normalizeShortAnswer`), plus the local `plainMath` and `closeEnough`. This check runs before the tutor model. Its verdict cannot be overruled. A rule `correct` returns fixed praise without calling the model (`service.ts:509-519`). A rule `incorrect` is final in a test (`service.ts:537-546`), uses up the prepared hints and reveals the solution on the third miss (`service.ts:547-568`), and when the tutor is called, `enforceTutorInvariants` still forces `incorrect` (`tutor.ts:128`). So each parsing or normalisation error becomes a wrong grade that no other layer catches. For a learning app this is the worst kind of defect. A child who answered correctly is told she is wrong and shown "the solution", which is what she typed. A child who answered wrongly is praised. The wrong result then feeds FSRS scheduling (practice and explain), test results and summaries (test), and Buddy's picture of what is "secure" (`buddy/state.ts:263`), so errors are not practised again before the exam.

Merges applied here: `repro-03` is folded into C-1 and `repro-04` into C-2 as runtime proof. The percent half of `p2-F2` is reported only under C-4, so `p2-F2` (C-3) covers the mixed-number half together with its member `p2-mixed-number-preview-vs-grader`. `repro-07` (now medium) and the PIN brute force (`repro-08`, now high) are not in this section.

---

### C-1 `numeric-key-three-decimals-misparsed` — Keys with exactly three decimals are read as thousands for de/fr/es/it learners

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** Answer a `numeric` question in practice, explain, test or homework help, for a learner whose locale is de, fr, es or it.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:134-141` (the key `item.answer` is parsed with the learner's locale) → `packages/shared-math/src/numeric-input.ts:68-73` (the `de` branch removes a dot followed by exactly three digits, line 71) → `service.ts:537-568` (a rule `incorrect` is final), `tutor.ts:128` (it overrides the model).
- **Current behavior:** Keys are stored with a decimal point: the prompts in `generate.ts:85` and `materials/extract.ts:90` ask for this, and `apps/mobile/lib/numbers.ts:2` documents it. `parseNumericInput('0.125','de')` returns 125, and likewise `0.625` gives 625, `1.250` gives 1250 and `2.375` gives 2375. The child's `0,125` or `1/8` parses to 0.125, so the check returns `incorrect`. The reverse also holds: the wrong answer `125` is graded `correct`, and so is `1` for the key `0.001`.
- **Expected behavior:** A key written with a point is read as a decimal. The learner's locale applies only to what the learner types. `0,125` is correct and `125` is wrong.
- **Problem:** The key is parsed with the learner-input parser. `accepted_answers` are never checked in the numeric branch. `plainNumber` (`evaluate.ts:182-183`) already treats `[.,]\d{3}$` as ambiguous, but `ruleCheck` returns before that guard is ever reached.
- **User impact:** A correct answer is rejected three times. The child is then shown "Die Lösung ist: 0,125" (the display uses `localDecimal`), which is exactly what she typed. The item is recorded as `revealed` (FSRS Again), and in a test it is `missed`. A child who types the wrong `125` is told "Stimmt – gut gemacht!".
- **Concrete scenario:** Topic "Brüche in Dezimalzahlen", with a numeric item whose key is `0.125`. Lena (de, 12) types `0,125`, which returns hint 1. She types `1/8`, which returns hint 2. She types `0,125` again and gets the worked solution with status `revealed`, due the next day. The same item in a Probetest is `missed`, and zero tutor calls are made.
- **Root cause:** The canonical key is parsed with a locale-dependent parser whose German thousands rule assumes that `.ddd` is a thousands separator.
- **Recommended fix:** In `ruleCheck`, parse `item.answer` canonically with `parseNumericInput(item.answer, 'en')` or a strict parser. Also check `accepted_answers` in the numeric branch. In the `de` branch, treat a lone dot followed by three digits (with no comma) as ambiguous and return `value: null`, which leads to `unknown`. Add tests with the keys `0.125`, `1.250` and `2.375` against `0,125`, `1/8` and `125` for all four comma locales, and check existing items with `answer ~ '^-?\d+\.\d{3}$'`.
- **Blast radius:** All numeric items with a three-decimal key (eighths, rounding to three places, π≈3.142, √2≈1.414, mm→m, molar masses) for four of the five locales, in every session mode, including FSRS state and test results.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced). The phase-1 reproduction `repro-03` and two independent second-opinion runs both went end to end on real Postgres with a 12-year-old de learner, and the failing paths made zero tutor calls.

---

### C-2 `tolerance-accepts-wrong-integers` — A 1 % tolerance grades wrong numeric answers as correct

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** Answer a `numeric` question in any mode and any locale.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:80-86` (`closeEnough`), `:141` (the numeric verdict) and `:200` (`differentNumber` reuses it) → `service.ts:509-519` (a rule `correct` means no model call) → `service.ts:790-818` (status `correct`, `first_try_correct`, FSRS Good).
- **Current behavior:** Any value within `max(1 % × |key|, 0.01 if |key| < 1)` is `correct`. Examples that pass: 242 for 240, 101 or 99 for 100, 1009 for 1000, 104 for 105, 750 for 753, `12,1` for 12, `0,02` for 0,01, `242 cm` for 240 cm, and even the expression `2*121` for 240. Nothing is written into `accepted_answers`, and no other part of the system ever reviews the answer again.
- **Expected behavior:** Exact integer arithmetic, dates and counts are compared exactly. A tolerance applies only where an item is declared as rounded or measured.
- **Problem:** A blanket tolerance carried over from the legacy spec (`docs/legacy`, which is not a requirement) is applied to every numeric key. Current docs do not mention it. `docs/architecture.md` §Practice says only "numbers with decimal comma and units", and the header comment of `evaluate.ts` names "tolerance" with no value. No test covers it.
- **User impact:** A child who computes 12·20 = 242 hears "Stimmt – gut gemacht!". The item closes as first-try correct and is scheduled days ahead. In a Probetest, wrong answers count as right and the topic is reported as secure.
- **Concrete scenario:** Numeric item "4 Stunden sind wie viele Minuten?" with key `240`, where Lena types `242`. The rule returns `correct` (`evaluated_by = 'rule'`) with no model call. `item_states` gets `last_outcome = 'first_try'`, due in 3 days. In test mode, 750 for 753, `242 cm` for 240 cm and 143 for 144 all come back correct, and the summary lists all three topics as `secure_topics`.
- **Root cause:** A single global relative tolerance meant for rounded decimals is applied to all numeric keys, including exact integers. Items carry no precision or tolerance field.
- **Recommended fix:** Compare exactly by default: integers must be equal, and a decimal key allows at most half a unit in its last written decimal place. Add an optional zod-validated `tolerance` or `rounded` field to the item contract (through a new migration), set only for π, roots and measured values. Apply the same rule in `differentNumber`. Add tests (242≠240, 1009≠1000, 12,1≠12) and document the rule in `docs/architecture.md` §Practice.
- **Blast radius:** All `numeric` items. Whole-number errors are accepted for keys ≥ 100, and decimal near misses at any size. `short` and `formula` items are affected only softly: their near misses go to the tutor instead of being flagged as wrong.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced) via `repro-04` and two second-opinion integration runs. One second opinion narrowed the finding: the tolerance was intentional in the legacy app for rounded results. Nothing in the rebuild limits it to rounded results, so the finding remains critical.

---

### C-3 `p2-F2` — The mixed number `3 1/2` is parsed as 31/2 = 15.5

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** Answer a `numeric` question by typing, using the math keys or speaking, then the rule check, then the hint ladder, the worked solution, or `missed` in a test.
- **Location:** `packages/shared-math/src/numeric-input.ts:72` (and `:77` for `en`), where all whitespace is removed before mathjs `evaluate` at `:82` → `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:136-141` → `service.ts:537-568`, `tutor.ts:128`. The typed preview at `apps/mobile/lib/math/typed.ts:313-317` keeps the gap and draws the mixed number.
- **Current behavior:** `parseNumericInput('3 1/2')` returns 15.5 in both de and en. Similarly `1 1/2 h` gives 5.5 and `-1 1/2` gives −5.5. For key `3.5`, the answer `3 1/2` is `incorrect`. For key `1.5` with unit h, `1 1/2 h` is `incorrect`. A mixed-number key (`3 1/2`) makes the correct `3,5` incorrect. `has_residue` is hard-coded to `false` (`:93`).
- **Expected behavior:** `3 1/2` is read as 3.5, or the parser returns `value: null` so the tutor judges.
- **Problem:** Deleting whitespace merges the whole number into the numerator. The guard in `plainNumber` ("3 1/2 is a mixed number, not 31/2", `evaluate.ts:178`) is used only by `differentNumber`, never for numeric items. The preview draws 3½, as `typed.test.ts` asserts, and the header of `typed.ts` claims the preview matches the checker, but the grader reads 15.5.
- **User impact:** A correct mixed-number answer (a core grade 5-6 notation) is rejected with "Noch nicht ganz". The hints are used up, the solution is revealed, the item goes to FSRS Again, and in a test it is `missed`. Buddy then plans more practice on something she already knows.
- **Concrete scenario:** Item "Schreibe 7/2 als gemischte Zahl" with key `3.5`. Lena types `3 1/2`. The preview shows 3½, but the verdict is `incorrect`, then hint 1, hint 2, and finally the worked solution with status `revealed`.
- **Root cause:** The separator normalisation removes all whitespace and hands the rest to a general expression evaluator. Mixed numbers are not recognised.
- **Recommended fix:** Before removing spaces, match `^(-?\d+)\s+(\d+)\/(\d+)$` (optionally followed by a unit) and compute whole ± num/den. If the input contains anything else that is ambiguous, return `value: null`. Add tests for `3 1/2`, `1 11/20`, `1 1/2 h` and `-1 1/2`.
- **Blast radius:** All numeric items (from photos, topics, tests and homework help), both for learner input and for stored keys, including FSRS state and test results.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced), through tsx runs of `parseNumericInput` and `ruleCheck` and an end-to-end API run on real Postgres.

---

### C-4 `p2-percent-answer-divided-by-100` — `25 %` is evaluated as 0.25 against the key 25

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** A numeric percent item (unit `%`), where the answer is typed as `25 %` or `25%`, or dictated as "fünfundzwanzig Prozent", followed by the numeric rule check.
- **Location:** `packages/shared-math/src/units.ts:4-79` (`UNIT_ALIASES` has no `%`) → `numeric-input.ts:64` (`stripTrailingUnit` leaves `%` in place) and `:82` (mathjs treats `%` as ÷100) → `evaluate.ts:139-141` (`given.unit` is null, so the unit guard is skipped) → `service.ts:537-568`. Related: `AnswerComposer.tsx` shows the unit `%` next to the field, and `voice/service.ts:25` writes units as symbols.
- **Current behavior:** For key `25` with unit `%`, both `25%` and `25 %` are `incorrect` and only the bare `25` is correct. For key `25 %` with no unit, `25` is `incorrect`. The third try replies "Die Lösung ist: 25 %".
- **Expected behavior:** A trailing `%` on an item whose unit is `%` is read as the unit, and both forms are accepted.
- **Problem:** The UI presents `%` as the unit, but the checker treats it as an operator. Voice mode always produces the failing form.
- **User impact:** A correct percentage is rejected by code, and the "solution" shown is what the child typed. Every spoken percent answer fails.
- **Concrete scenario:** Grade-6 Prozentrechnung: "Wie viel Prozent sind 1/4?", key `25`, unit `%`. Lena types `25%`, gets `incorrect` and then a prepared hint, and after the third try "Die Lösung ist: 25 %". In a test the item is `missed` with no model call.
- **Root cause:** `%` is missing from the unit aliases, and mathjs's percent semantics apply without anyone noticing.
- **Recommended fix:** Treat a trailing `%`, `Prozent`, `percent` (and the other locales' words) as the unit `%` in `stripTrailingUnit`, and compare values in that unit. Add tests for `25%`, `25 %` and the spoken form.
- **Blast radius:** Every numeric percent item in all locales, typed or spoken.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced) through tsx runs of `ruleCheck`. The percent half of `p2-F2` is the same defect and is merged here.

---

### C-5 `p2-latex-mixed-key-plainmath-31-2` — The LaTeX key `$3\frac{1}{2}$` is flattened to `31/2`

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** A short, numeric or formula item whose key or accepted answer is a gap-less LaTeX mixed number, then `ruleCheck` and `differentNumber`, then the verdict.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:58` (`\frac{a}{b}` becomes `a/b` with no separator from the whole number before it), `:147-151` (exact match), `:177-187` (`plainNumber`) and `:194-201` (`differentNumber`) → `service.ts:495-497` (`differentNumber` turns `unknown` into `incorrect`). The format comes from `items.ts:16` (`MATH_RULES` asks for LaTeX). By contrast, `valuesIn` (`evaluate.ts:209`) and the mobile renderer correctly read this key as 3½.
- **Current behavior:** `plainMath('$3\frac{1}{2}$')` gives `31/2` and `plainMath('$1\frac{11}{20}$')` gives `111/20`. For a short key `$3\frac{1}{2}$`, `31/2` is `correct`, while `3,5` and `7/2` are `incorrect` for sure. For the key `$1\frac{11}{20}$`, `1,55` is `incorrect`.
- **Expected behavior:** A whole number followed by `\frac` means whole + fraction. Equal values in another form are never ruled wrong for sure, and `31/2` is never ruled correct.
- **Problem:** The child sees 3½ but is graded against 15.5. The leak check (`valuesIn`) and the grader disagree about the same key.
- **User impact:** In fraction lessons, the reference persona's core topic, right answers written as an improper fraction or a decimal are rejected with no model involved, while the nonsense answer `31/2` gets "Stimmt – gut gemacht!" and FSRS Good.
- **Concrete scenario:** "Berechne $1\frac{1}{4} + 2\frac{1}{4}$" with key `$3\frac{1}{2}$`. Lena types `7/2` and gets `incorrect` by rule, which is final in a test and in practice leads to hints and then a reveal. On "Schreibe $\frac{7}{2}$ als gemischte Zahl" she types `31/2` and gets `correct`.
- **Root cause:** The `plainMath` regex drops the implicit "+" between a whole number and the `\frac` that follows it.
- **Recommended fix:** In `plainMath`, rewrite `(\d)\s*\\[dt]?frac` as `$1 ` followed by the fraction, as `valuesIn` already does. Make `plainNumber` accept mixed numbers (together with C-3). Add evaluate tests with mixed LaTeX keys.
- **Blast radius:** Every item whose key or accepted answer contains a gap-less LaTeX mixed number, from photo extraction or topic generation. Keys written with a gap (`3\,\frac{1}{2}`) are not affected.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced), through tsx runs and an end-to-end test-mode run through the real API.

---

### C-6 `p2-F1` — Exact match ignores operators and signs: `x=5`, `3,4` and `x^2-2x` are graded correct

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** Answer a short, formula or long question with a math key (typed or spoken), then the rule check, then "Stimmt – gut gemacht!", then FSRS, the summary and Buddy's STATE.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:147-151` (`targets.includes(normalizeShortAnswer(plainMath(text)))` means `correct`) ← `packages/shared-math/src/normalize.ts:13` (every non-letter, non-digit character becomes a space) ← `evaluate.ts:56-70` (`plainMath`). Downstream: `service.ts:509-519` (no tutor call), `:790-818` (first try, FSRS Good), `:388-399` (the summary lists the topic as secure).
- **Current behavior:** Verified with tsx: for the formula key `x^{2}+2x`, `x^2-2x` is correct; `x=5` is correct for `x=-5`; `x>3` is correct for `x<3`. For the short key `\frac{3}{4}`, `3,4`, `3-4` and `3:4` are all correct. `5` is correct for `-5`, and `3/4` is correct for `-\frac{3}{4}`. `normalizeShortAnswer` maps both `x^2+2x` and `x^2-2x` to `x 2 2x`.
- **Expected behavior:** `docs/architecture.md` §Practice allows rule verdicts only "where exactness is decidable". An exact match must keep operators, signs, relation symbols and separators. Anything else returns `unknown` so the tutor decides.
- **Problem:** A prose normaliser built for token overlap (`normalize.ts:4-7`) is used as the equality test for math. The formula-specific compact compare (`evaluate.ts:168-172`) is only reached after the operator-blind match has already returned.
- **User impact:** The most common grade 6-9 errors (sign errors, inequality direction, `3,4` for 3/4) are praised. The item closes as first-try correct, is scheduled far ahead, is listed under "Sitzt" and is reported to Buddy as secure, so it is never practised again before the exam.
- **Concrete scenario:** "Kürze 6/8" with key `$\frac{3}{4}$` (the fixture in `modes.int.test.ts`). Lena types `3,4`. She gets `correct` and "Stimmt – gut gemacht!", `first_try_correct = true`, FSRS Good, and `secure_topics` contains "Kürzen".
- **Root cause:** The wrong normaliser is used for the job: a prose tokeniser serves as the correctness oracle for math.
- **Recommended fix:** For keys or answers that contain digits or operators, compare a canonical form that preserves the math (keep `+ − = < > ≤ ≥ / ^ ·` and the decimal separator, normalise the Unicode minus and superscripts). Otherwise return `unknown`. Use the prose normaliser only for keys that contain neither digits nor operators. Add negative tests for the pairs listed above.
- **Blast radius:** Every short, formula or long item with a math key, from photos, topics, explain, tests and homework help (`help_solved`). Also affected: FSRS state, test results, and Buddy's planning based on `secure_topics`.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced), through tsx runs of `ruleCheck` and an end-to-end test-mode run (`3/4` for `-\frac{3}{4}` came back `correct` with no tutor call).

---

### C-7 `p2-NM-01` — Exact match ignores capitalisation, ß/ss and punctuation, so wrong spellings are graded correct

- **Severity:** Critical.
- **Affected user flow:** Answer a short, vocab or long item in German spelling (s/ss/ß, capitalisation, commas) or English capitalisation, then `ruleCheck`, then "Stimmt – gut gemacht!", then first try, then FSRS Good, then "Sitzt" and Buddy's STATE.
- **Location:** `packages/shared-math/src/normalize.ts:8-16` (`toLowerCase` at `:11`, `ß→ss` at `:12`, punctuation to space at `:13`) → `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:147-151` → `service.ts:509-519` (no tutor call; in homework help the reply is "Stark – das hast du selbst gelöst!") → `:790-818` + `fsrs.ts:54` (Good) → `service.ts:392` and `buddy/state.ts:263` (secure).
- **Current behavior:** For short items, `ss` is correct for the key `ß`, and so are `Strasse` for `Straße`, `weiss` for `weiß`, `Masse` for `Maße`, `das laufen` for `das Laufen`, `l` for `L`, and "Ich glaube dass er kommt" for "Ich glaube, dass er kommt.". `evaluate.ts` even says elsewhere that "ß stays (it is a letter of its own)", but the folding happens earlier.
- **Expected behavior:** Case, ß and punctuation count in the `correct` comparison. Folded matches are at most a near miss or `unknown` for the tutor.
- **Problem:** The folding erases exactly the features these exercises test. No item field exists for case-sensitive or exact matching, and no test covers ß, case or punctuation.
- **User impact:** On a photographed spelling sheet ("s, ss oder ß?", "Groß oder klein?", "Setze die Kommas"), every wrong answer on the feature being tested is praised and shown with a green "Richtig". Buddy then considers the topic secure before the Diktat.
- **Concrete scenario:** Sheet item "Setze ein: s, ss oder ß? Stra_e" with key `ß`. Lena types `ss`. She gets `correct` and "Stimmt – gut gemacht!", status `correct` on the first try, FSRS Good, and `secure_topics` contains "s-Laute".
- **Root cause:** A prose normaliser (case folding, ß folding, punctuation stripping) is the correctness oracle for every written kind.
- **Recommended fix:** For `correct`, only trim, collapse whitespace and apply NFC. Use the folded forms only to detect a named near miss or to return `unknown` for the tutor. Add tests for `ss`/`ß`, `l`/`L` and a sentence missing its comma.
- **Blast radius:** All short, vocab and long items (from photos, typed lists, Buddy sets, tests and homework) in German spelling, English capitalisation and every punctuation task. Also affected: FSRS state, summaries and Buddy's STATE.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced). The rule verdicts were produced by executing the real normalise and match logic. The effect on the HTTP path (no tutor call, first try, FSRS Good) is code-traced through `service.ts`.

---

## 5. High-Priority Findings

This section lists 37 high-severity findings. Each one breaks a main flow for a real group of users, breaks a documented promise (privacy, contact, PIN gate) or loses data. None reaches the critical bar of §4, either because it needs a narrower trigger or because a workaround exists.

| Area                          | Count |
| ----------------------------- | ----- |
| Answer checking               | 6     |
| Homework help                 | 5     |
| Practice sessions & materials | 5     |
| Identity, PIN & onboarding    | 6     |
| Account deletion & retention  | 4     |
| Auth resilience & offline     | 2     |
| Contact & reminders           | 3     |
| Child safety                  | 2     |
| Data location & processors    | 2     |
| Deployment                    | 1     |
| Rendering                     | 1     |

Merges applied:

- The PIN brute-force reproductions `repro-08`, `put-pin-no-lockout` and `pin-change-without-lockout` are one entry (H-17).
- `repro-05` → H-7, `repro-12` → H-13, `repro-09` → H-18, `repro-10` → H-20, `repro-11` → H-21, `repro-24` → H-23, `repro-14` → H-25, `repro-26` → H-28, `repro-02` → H-29, `repro-15` → H-37. Each repro is cited as the runtime proof.
- `p2-T7` → H-24. The materials half of `p2-xs-delete-is-soft-archive-content-kept` → H-26.
- `agreed-reminder-vanishes` is raised to high (H-37), because `repro-15` reproduced the pause-while-deferred and quiet-hours variants, not only the outage case.
- `repro-20` (offline sign-out leaves the push token active) is reported in §6 at medium, merged into `p2-push-device-binding-first-device-forever`.
- `repro-07` (the PIN reset through a fresh sign-in) is medium and narrowed; see §6.

Line numbers were spot-checked against HEAD `62a6028`.

### Answer checking

### H-1 `p2-T1` — A numeric answer that just retypes the task is marked correct by rule

- **Severity:** High (originally critical; narrowed because the value really is equal and the child has to retype the task).
- **Affected user flow:** Answering a numeric question in practice, Probetest or homework help → `ruleCheck` → "Stimmt – gut gemacht!" or "selbst gelöst" → FSRS and summary.
- **Location:** `packages/shared-math/src/numeric-input.ts:82` (mathjs `evaluate` runs on the learner's text); `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:133-141`; `apps/api/src/modules/practice/service.ts:509-518` (a rule `correct` skips the tutor); `apps/mobile/components/math/MathKeys.tsx` (the ·, /, ², √ keys); `packages/shared-math/src/__tests__/numeric-input.test.ts:50-60` makes expression evaluation the intended behaviour.
- **Current behavior:** The learner's text is evaluated as arithmetic. If the value equals the key, the rule returns `correct` and no model is asked. Examples: `17·23` for 391, `√144` for 12, `1/2+1/4` for 0.75. The repo's own fixtures (`modes.int.test.ts:240-242`, `hints.int.test.ts:103-105`) can be solved this way.
- **Expected behavior:** Rules decide only "where exactness is decidable" (`docs/architecture.md` §Practice). An answer that contains an operator the key does not have is not a computed result. It should go to the tutor, or be treated as not yet computed.
- **Problem:** The rule compares values, not what the learner produced, and the math keys make retyping the task trivial.
- **User impact:** A 12-year-old enters "Berechne 17 · 23" with the keys and is praised. The item counts as correct on the first try, FSRS schedules it days out, the topic shows as "sitzt", and a homework task counts as "selbst gelöst".
- **Concrete scenario:** Key 391. Lena types `17·23` → `parseNumericInput` gives 391 → `closeEnough` → `correct` → FSRS Good.
- **Root cause:** A general evaluator (added for the math keys) is combined with "same value means correct".
- **Recommended fix:** In `ruleCheck`, if the learner text contains an operator and the key is a plain number, return `unknown` so the tutor judges. Add `evaluate.test` cases that must not grade as correct (`17·23`→391, `1/2+1/4`→0.75).
- **Blast radius:** Every numeric item in every mode and every locale.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced). The finding's own example 0.875 is instead misgraded by the critical three-decimal key bug (§4 `numeric-key-three-decimals-misparsed`).

### H-2 `p2-en-comma-three-decimals-answer-misgraded` — For English-profile learners, "0,125" is read as 125 and marked wrong for good

- **Severity:** High (originally critical; narrowed to learners with an `en` profile who type a decimal comma).
- **Affected user flow:** Answering a numeric question in practice, explain or test mode with profile language `en`.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:135` (every non-de/fr/es/it locale maps to `en`); `packages/shared-math/src/numeric-input.ts:76` (in `en`, a comma before exactly three digits is removed); `service.ts:547-566` (hint ladder, then the worked solution).
- **Current behavior:** `parseNumericInput('0,125','en')` is 125, so the rule says `incorrect` against key 0.125. The rule verdict is final: `enforceTutorInvariants` never overrules `incorrect`. A shorter comma decimal (`2,5`) gives `unknown`, so only three decimals are affected.
- **Expected behavior:** A correct value is graded correct, or left to the tutor. A leading `0,` can never be a thousands separator.
- **Problem:** The decimal convention is taken from the UI language, not from the input.
- **User impact:** A child is marked wrong three times for a correct answer, then shown a "solution" equal to what they typed. FSRS records Again.
- **Concrete scenario:** Tom (en profile, school in Germany) types `0,125` on the iOS keyboard → 125 ≠ 0.125 → hint 1, hint 2, worked solution.
- **Root cause:** In the `en` branch, any comma followed by three digits is removed.
- **Recommended fix:** Treat a single separator followed by exactly three digits as ambiguous in every locale (`unknown`). Never read a thousands separator after `0` or `-0`. Add an int test for `en` + `0,125`. The fix proposed in §4 for the de/fr/es/it key does not cover this case.
- **Blast radius:** `en` learners who write decimal commas; every key with three decimals.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced).

### H-3 `p2-NM-02` — `missing_word` only compares positions: "5" for key "x = 5" gets "Fast" and a missed test item

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** A short or vocab item whose key has two or more tokens → `missing_word` → "Fast – da fehlt noch ein Wort" → partially_correct (test: `missed`; practice: FSRS Hard).
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:153-158` (line 156: `words(t).slice(1).join(' ') === norm`); `normalize.ts:13` (strips `=`, `-`, `.`); `service.ts:520-536`, `:797-803`.
- **Current behavior:** Any answer equal to the key minus its first token is a "near miss". Key `x = 5`: both `5` and `-5` are near misses. Key `minus 3`: `3` is a near miss. Key `not bad`: `bad` is a near miss.
- **Expected behavior:** `docs/architecture.md:403` limits the rule to "a missing first word such as the article". `5` is right, and `-5` or `bad` are wrong.
- **Problem:** The rule drops any first token (a variable name, a sign word, a negation) with no model involved.
- **User impact:** In a Probetest, a correct `5` for "Löse 2x = 10" is recorded as missed. In practice she must type `x = 5`, which costs the first-try credit and moves FSRS to Hard. Wrong answers are praised as "almost".
- **Concrete scenario:** Test item, short, key `x = 5`. She types `5` → `missing_word` → `missed`, and the summary says "Noch nicht richtig".
- **Root cause:** A positional rule runs on punctuation-stripped text, where operators have become "words".
- **Recommended fix:** Apply `missing_word` only to vocab items whose first key token is an article or particle. Never apply it when the key contains digits, operators or `=`.
- **Blast radius:** Short and vocab items with multi-token keys: tests, FSRS ratings, summaries, Buddy's shaky topics.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced). A right answer is misgraded only for short keys with a label prefix and no accepted variant. The prompts ask for accepted variants, which limits how often this happens.

### H-4 `p2-NM-03` — Short keys with digits call a wrong number a "typo" and print the solution on the first try

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Practice or explain: a short or vocab answer → `typo` → "Fast – so schreibt man es: {{answer}}" → she copies it → correct with help.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:50-53` (`allowedSlips` counts digits too) and `:159-166` (the typo check runs before `differentNumber`); `service.ts:520-536` (the reply prints `plainMath(item.answer)` with `revealed:false`); `service.ts:704-719` (the "never the solution before the second hint" guard covers only model verdicts).
- **Current behavior:** These pairs of key and answer are all classed as `typo`, and each reply shows the full key: `14:35 Uhr` vs `15:35 Uhr`, `1250 m` vs `1350 m`, `24 cm²` vs `42 cm²`, `a = 12 cm` vs `a = 13 cm`.
- **Expected behavior:** A different number or time is a wrong answer and goes through the hint ladder. Showing the solution counts as revealed (`docs/architecture.md:410`, "revealed → Again").
- **Problem:** Edit distance cannot separate a slip from a different number. The typo path bypasses both the reveal guard and the `revealed` flag.
- **User impact:** She gets a time calculation wrong, is told it is a spelling issue, sees the answer, copies it, and it counts as "correct with help" (Hard) instead of revealed (Again).
- **Concrete scenario:** Key `14:35 Uhr`. She types `15:35 Uhr` → "Fast – so schreibt man es: 14:35 Uhr" → she types `14:35 Uhr` → with_help.
- **Root cause:** `allowedSlips` counts characters, not letters, and the typo branch runs first.
- **Recommended fix:** Skip `typo` when the key or the answer contains digits. When a typo reply prints the key, set `revealed=true`.
- **Blast radius:** Short and vocab items in practice and explain mode; FSRS; summaries.
- **Confidence:** Partly confirmed. The digit case is reproduced. Real-word slips (`Stadt`/`statt`) follow the documented design and are not counted as the defect.

### H-5 `p2-NM-04` — A spoken choice letter collides with a one-letter option

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Voice mode, multiple choice: the options are read as "A: the, B: a, C: an" → she says "A" → rule verdict.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:95-106` (the exact option-text match at :99-100 wins over the letter rule at :102-106), `:123-126`; `apps/mobile/lib/speech/spoken.ts:27-37`; `apps/mobile/app/practice/[id].tsx:743-749`.
- **Current behavior:** `choiceNamed('A', ['the','a','an'])` returns 1 (the option "a"), not 0. The right choice is ruled `incorrect` without a model.
- **Expected behavior:** A single letter that is both a badge letter and an option text is ambiguous. It should go to the tutor, or the badge letter should win in voice mode.
- **Problem:** The app teaches her to answer with badge letters, and the code prefers the option text.
- **User impact:** She says the right letter and gets "Noch nicht ganz". In a test the item is missed; after three misses she gets the worked solution and FSRS Again.
- **Concrete scenario:** Test item "\_\_\_ sun is hot", choices [the, a, an], correct 0. She says "A" → index 1 → `missed`.
- **Root cause:** Option text and badge letter share one namespace, and the letter loses.
- **Recommended fix:** In `choiceNamed`, return `null` when a single letter maps to two different indices. Add tests for `['the','a','an']` and `['c','a','b']`.
- **Blast radius:** Voice-mode multiple choice with one-letter options: English articles, Spanish y/e/o/u/a, geometry sides a/b/c.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced).

### H-6 `mathjs-evaluate-unbounded-input` — The full mathjs evaluator runs on learner text; one answer can crash the API process

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** POST `/practice/sessions/:id/answer` for any numeric item.
- **Location:** `packages/shared-math/src/numeric-input.ts:80-86` (`evaluate(numericText)`); `apps/api/src/modules/practice/evaluate.ts:133-141`; input cap of 2000 characters only (`packages/shared-types/src/contracts/learning.ts:251`); no rate limiter in `apps/api/src/app.ts`.
- **Current behavior:** Matrix and range constructors are evaluated. Measured with a 1 GB heap (same as `vercel.json`): `ones(9000,9000)` took 1.6 s and 683 MB; `range(1,1e8)` took 8.9 s and 1.7 GB; `zeros(20000,20000)` ended in a fatal out-of-memory crash. Forms with commas are neutralised for de/fr/es/it learners, but comma-free forms such as `1:6e7` are not.
- **Expected behavior:** A numeric field accepts a small arithmetic subset with bounded cost.
- **Problem:** An unrestricted evaluator runs on untrusted input in the request path, before any model budget applies.
- **User impact:** Any signed-in learner, or a child typing nonsense, can stall or kill the function instance. Other learners' requests in flight fail.
- **Concrete scenario:** She types `1:6e7` into a numeric field and taps Prüfen → the heap is exhausted → the process exits.
- **Root cause:** mathjs `evaluate` is used with no node allowlist and no length cap.
- **Recommended fix:** Parse with `mathjs.parse` and allow only Constant, Operator and Parenthesis nodes, `sqrt`/`abs`, and `pi`. Cap numeric input at about 64 characters. Add a per-account rate limit.
- **Blast radius:** The API instance serving the request, and all learners on it.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced).

### Homework help

### H-7 `homework-sheet-practice-dead-end` — The homework help session is reachable only for 12 h; afterwards the sheet's "Üben" button falsely says there are no tasks

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Homework photo → help session → coming back later via home or "Mein Stoff".
- **Location:** `apps/mobile/components/library/MaterialCard.tsx:112-122` ("Üben" for every ready material; `purpose` is never read); `apps/mobile/app/library.tsx:144`; `apps/api/src/modules/practice/selection.ts:54` (`i.origin <> 'homework'`); `apps/api/src/modules/buddy/home.ts:137-141` (the resume card only within 12 h of `started_at` and while `answered < total`); `buddy/state.ts:184` (only the 5 newest sessions are loaded); `MaterialView.session_id` (`packages/shared-types/src/contracts/learning.ts:68`) is never used by the app; `apps/mobile/locales/de/errors.json:28`.
- **Current behavior:** After 12 h, or after five newer sessions, nothing leads back to the still-active help session. "Üben" on the sheet returns 404 `no_questions`, and the app shows "Dafür gibt es noch keine Aufgaben. Fotografier zuerst ein Arbeitsblatt." right under a card that says "N Aufgaben". Pages added later join the unreachable session (`materials/service.ts:528-547`).
- **Expected behavior:** A homework sheet leads to its help session (the contract carries `session_id` and `purpose` for this purpose), or hides "Üben". The error must not claim there are no tasks.
- **Problem:** No screen opens a help session by material, and the resume window is fixed at 12 h.
- **User impact:** Homework started in the evening cannot be continued the next afternoon. The only way back is to photograph it again.
- **Concrete scenario:** `repro-05`: resume card present at 0 h and 11 h, gone at 13 h. The session is still `active` with one open task, and POST `/practice/sessions {material_id}` returns 404 `no_questions`.
- **Root cause:** `MaterialCard` renders the same actions for every material, selection excludes homework, and nothing reads `session_id`.
- **Recommended fix:** For `purpose=homework`, show "Weiter mit der Hausaufgabe", which opens `/practice/${session_id}`. On the server, return the help session for a homework `material_id`. Drop or lengthen the 12 h cut for mode `help`.
- **Blast radius:** Every homework material after its first 12 h; every library visit.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced; `repro-05`).

### H-8 `beenden-finishes-for-good` — "Beenden" finishes the session for good although the copy says the answers are kept

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Leaving a running session (practice, explain, test, help) with the header button "Beenden".
- **Location:** `apps/mobile/app/practice/[id].tsx:283-297` (`close()` calls `finishSession` unconditionally, at :288); `apps/api/src/modules/practice/service.ts:1045-1056` (status `finished`, no way back); `apps/mobile/locales/de/practice.json:6` (`end_hint`: "Deine Antworten bleiben gespeichert."); `materials/service.ts:530-540` (later pages join only an active session).
- **Current behavior:** The only in-app exit ends the session. Open questions can never be answered in it again: answering returns 409 "Session has ended". For homework, the remaining tasks become unreachable; the home shows "Hausaufgabe geschafft" for 30 minutes, then nothing.
- **Expected behavior:** Leaving with open questions pauses the session (the server already resumes active sessions), or a confirmation states that the open questions will be dropped.
- **Problem:** Pause semantics were promised in the copy and in a code comment but never built.
- **User impact:** Homework with 5 tasks, 2 solved: she taps "Beenden" to answer a message, and tasks 3–5 are gone. In a test, the untouched questions count against her.
- **Concrete scenario:** Help session with 5 tasks, 2 answered → "Beenden" → finished → `practice_result` for 30 minutes → null; library "Üben" → 404.
- **Root cause:** "Beenden" is wired to the terminal finish endpoint, with no confirmation.
- **Recommended fix:** Navigate back without finishing while questions are open, or show a confirmation with the number of open questions. Never finish a help session that has open tasks. Fix `end_hint`.
- **Blast radius:** Every session mode; homework help is hit hardest.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced).

### H-9 `p2-HW-01` — The homework solution leaks when the tutor over-praises an intermediate step

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Answering in a help session (mode `help`).
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/service.ts:635` (`givesAwayHomework` runs on the raw decision), `:658-666` (the demotion comes later and changes only the verdict); `apps/api/src/modules/practice/tutor.ts:156-161` (`if (d.verdict === 'correct') return false`).
- **Current behavior:** When the tutor says `correct` and its reply states the final result, the leak check passes. The code then demotes the verdict to `partially_correct`, because her text does not contain the key, but keeps the reply. The item stays open with the solution in the stored reply, and `revealed=false`.
- **Expected behavior:** Demote first, then run the leak check. In help mode, no reply may contain the key unless the item closes as correct.
- **Problem:** Two invariants are applied in the wrong order.
- **User impact:** The child gets the homework answer from Buddy, the very thing the mode promises never happens, and the app cannot tell that it did.
- **Concrete scenario:** Key 11/12. She types "Hauptnenner 12". The tutor replies "Super … = 11/12" with verdict correct → demoted to "Fast" with 11/12 still shown.
- **Root cause:** `service.ts:635` checks before the homework-specific demotion.
- **Recommended fix:** Compute `homeworkSolved` first and pass the demoted verdict into `givesAwayHomework`, then run the repair round.
- **Blast radius:** Every help session with non-`long` items; triggered by model output, but deterministic once it occurs.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced with a scripted tutor reply). The related variant `p2-sec-homework-leak-check-skipped-on-correct` (with `p2-T3`, the guard defeated when the child lists candidates) is in §6.

### H-10 `p2-HW-02` — A correct homework answer written differently from the key cannot close the task

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Help session answer → closing the task.
- **Location:** `apps/api/src/modules/practice/service.ts:658-666`; `apps/api/src/modules/practice/tutor.ts:196-199` (`homeworkSolved` is a normalised substring check; `accepted_answers` are ignored); `apps/mobile/app/practice/[id].tsx:521-526` (no skip in help mode).
- **Current behavior:** `0,875` for key `$\frac{7}{8}$`, `5` for `x = 5`, or `12` for `12 cm²`: the tutor says correct, the code demotes to "Fast", the reply "Genau, richtig!" stands under the "Fast" chip, and the task stays open. Every retry gives the same result.
- **Expected behavior:** Compare by value (and by accepted answers) before falling back to a substring check. The shown verdict must not contradict the reply (rule 5).
- **Problem:** A string check overrides the model's correct verdict, and there is no way to move past the task.
- **User impact:** She has the right answer, Buddy says so in words, the app asks again, and the rest of the sheet is blocked behind this task (see H-11).
- **Concrete scenario:** The extractor stores the key as LaTeX (`MATH_RULES`, `items.ts:16`). She types `0,875` → rule `unknown` → tutor says correct → `homeworkSolved` false → partially_correct, twice.
- **Root cause:** `homeworkSolved` compares strings, not values.
- **Recommended fix:** Compare values first (parse both sides, check `accepted_answers`). When demoting, replace the reply with a nudge.
- **Blast radius:** Homework items whose key is written differently from what a 12-year-old types (fractions vs decimals, `x =`, units).
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced).

### H-11 `p2-HW-03` — Help mode has no way past an open task except "Beenden"

- **Severity:** High
- **Affected user flow:** Moving between tasks in a help session.
- **Location:** `apps/mobile/app/practice/[id].tsx:152-159` (the shown item is the first open one), `:521-526` (skip and hint are undefined), `:588-593` (flagging not allowed), `:762-768` ("Weiter" only after the item is closed); `apps/api/src/modules/practice/service.ts:861` (`no_hints`), `:946` (`reveal_not_allowed`), `:1000` (`flag_not_allowed`).
- **Current behavior:** She sees the first open task and cannot skip, get a hint, flag it or move on. The only remaining control, "Beenden", ends the session (H-8).
- **Expected behavior:** She can set a task aside without anything being revealed, and get help with the next one.
- **Problem:** The practice-mode controls were removed for help mode, and nothing homework-appropriate replaced them.
- **User impact:** Stuck on task 1, she cannot get help with tasks 2–6. Leaving destroys the session, and the summary then says "Hausaufgabe geschafft".
- **Concrete scenario:** Six tasks; she does not understand the first word problem, taps "Beenden", and tasks 2–6 are unreachable.
- **Root cause:** Every progression control is gated off for help mode.
- **Recommended fix:** Add a help-mode "Später" that moves the open pointer without closing the item (server: mark the item deferred). Stop "Beenden" from finishing an unfinished help session.
- **Blast radius:** Every help session.
- **Confidence:** Confirmed (runtime-reproduced).
